# Package report: DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII

The package contains `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/README.md`, `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/Suggested.lean`, `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/metadata.toml` and `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/sources.toml`. The README expands all 68 accepted targets in six layers, retaining their APIs, every Check and the worked examples. The generated skeleton was made from `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/design/plan.json` before the prose expansion. Only the top-level `gaps` list of that JSON changed; parsed comparison against the repository input confirms that every other member is identical.

The separate `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/design/review.json` and pre-existing reports were absent. The acceptance evidence available in the inputs is the embedded `review` object in `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/design/plan.json`, marked accepted and containing an entry for every target. No instruction from fetched text was executed, and no suspicious instruction was encountered.

## Headline inventory and acceptance signatures

This inventory was written before `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/Suggested.lean`. “Stated” means a headline signature with an honest `sorry`, not a claim that its proof has been formalized.

| Headline | Status |
|---|---|
| Nonvanishing of ordinary and alternating MZVs | Stated: `multipleZeta_ne_zero` |
| Brown generation, ordinary and alternating | Stated: `brown_spanning`, `alternating_brown_spanning` |
| Equality of zeta and CMPL weight spaces | Stated: `value_space_bridges` |
| Strong Brown generation of alternating CMPLs | Stated: `strong_brown_spanning` |
| Anderson–Thakur interpolation | Not statable at the pins with a few simple definitions: lacks the constructed H-polynomial family, Ω and the chosen Carlitz period |
| Entire deformation and Frobenius specialisation | Not statable: lacks the arithmetic E/C∞ and analytic twist/evaluation interfaces |
| Chang's general MZ-property descent and separation of weights | Not statable: lacks the actual MZ-property analytic witness carrier; its algebraic-closure basis consequence is separately stated below |
| ABP independence criterion and Eulerian suffix constraint | Not statable: lacks the merged analytic prefix system and period realisation |
| Dimension of the complete X_w | Stated: `frobenius_solution_dimension`, on its exact descended polynomial carrier; §4.3 gives the linear identification with the original R-valued space |
| General character-twisted system rigidity | Not statable: lacks the constructed decorated analytic prefix system; no arbitrary support or fake existence predicate replaces it |
| Ngo Dac's two-row theorem, including the Eulerian endpoint | Stated: `two_row_rigidity`, using native coefficient Frobenius and the exact explicit H_w in this range |
| Small-entry independence and bounded q-indivisible independence | Stated: `small_entry_independent`, `q_indivisible_independent`; the small-weight basis assertions are included in the unrestricted Thakur basis signature |
| CMPL basis on IND_w | Stated: `cmpl_ind_basis` |
| Thakur basis and Todd dimension formula | Stated: `thakur_basis`, `todd_dimension` |
| Thakur basis over K̄ | Stated: `thakur_basis_algebraicClosure`, with both compatible scalar towers explicit |
| Complete fixed-weight and mixed-weight relation kernels | Not statable: lacks the constructed q-shuffle rewriting operator, not merely a finite span or abstract kernel carrier |
| Independence of roots belonging to distinct degree characters | Stated: `character_roots_independent`, with injective character list and compatible constant-field embeddings |
| Period character decomposition and unique normalized ACMPL relation | Not statable: lacks the normalized period realisation, Ω and π̃ |
| Hoffman-like alternating basis and alternating dimension formula | Stated: `alternating_hoffman_basis`, `alternating_dimension` |

The 16 data definitions have real bodies on native carriers and are used by the 18 headline statements. No API lemma, admitted example, bounded Frobenius stand-in, arbitrary proposition field, heavy data definition, extra import or option override was added. The Frobenius residual is an honest linear map with proved addition and scalar laws. Its function coordinates outside the finite prefix tree are forced to zero, and polynomial degrees are unbounded. Thus its kernel is the full descended solution space, not a bounded approximation. The counting definitions use finite composition counts, and README §0.11–§0.12 states their full recurrence characterization.

## Corrections and limits of support

- Corrected the finite-place attribution: CM21 Theorem 1.2.2 supplies the weightwise linear map, not an algebra homomorphism. CM21 lines 169–173 explicitly discuss multiplicativity as a further question. The needed multiplicativity and principal-ideal kernel are CCM-ALG Theorem 1.2.3, CCM-ALG lines 176–184. The bound and its generality are unchanged.
- Corrected the unbound quotient m in the coefficient-system proof: write ℓ=mq+n, 0≤n<q. The root block has ℓ unknowns, the child block ℓ−m, total 2ℓ−m; at q=3,ℓ=7,m=2 this is 12, not 10. This also corrects the reversed block counts in the embedded review's commentary.
- Made collapse total on its positive-index domain: T_i(∅)=∅ and h=min(i,depth) for nonempty inputs. The raw empty formula would create a zero part. Defined the full CMPL domain D_s by the quantified term-decay condition, and retained the larger-than-polydisc example. Explicitly retained the root in a prefix tree with an empty pair family.
- Specified degree zero as −∞; natural degree is used only after monicity excludes zero. Specified empty-list defaults and excluded q=1, the zero ring, zero index entries, a zero coefficient denominator and a zero ABP determinant.
- Replaced numerical-check narratives by exact discriminants: at q=3, S₁(4) begins 2θ⁻⁶ while ℓ₁⁻⁴ begins θ⁻¹², so Li(4)−ζ(4) begins θ⁻⁶. The d=0 fundamental and next-weight identities give 1−1=0, whereas omitting D₁ gives 1+ℓ₁⁻¹≠0.
- Carried the native completion's equality instance explicitly in every signature that needs it, rather than adding a custom instance or option. Removed it from the denominator definition, which does not need it.
- Added finite generation explicitly to the X_w dimension signature: native natural finrank also returns zero on an infinite-dimensional space, so finrank zero alone would weaken the nondivisible-weight conclusion.
- Specified the exact polynomial presentation of X_w, its zero extension and restriction, its θ versus t convention, and its identification with the original space by descent. The two-row theorem is stated after one forward twist, with the permitted nonzero endpoint retained.
- Added scalar-tower compatibility in the K̄-basis and constant-root signatures. Retained distinct characters, nonzero supported coefficients, suffix independence, nonvanishing points, termwise bounds for full matrices, and polynomial versus rational-solution distinctions.
- Converted the generic harmonic product to an exact supplier contract over integer structure constants. A rational-coefficient word algebra cannot be specialized to characteristic p through a nonexistent ring map ℚ→K.

The accepted design's fixes remain: empty truncations at every integer cutoff; the missing t−θ subdiagonal; weak tuple convergence only for prefix columns; collected support in the Eulerian-tail assertion; induction on normalized smaller-weight suffix relations; the positive-degree condition in monic descent; the q=3,w=4 nonzero two-row endpoint; and simultaneous mixed/equal-degree induction for Thakur's higher-depth products (THAKUR-UPDATES entry 36, THAKUR-UPDATES line 305).

The principal unsupported supplier is **DrinfeldModulesAndTModules itself**. The supplied content main, board-package search and open-head lookup contain no roadmap by that name; the exact lookup returned `no roadmap DrinfeldModulesAndTModules on main or in an open roadmap pull request`. Its name does occur as a consumer in GlobalShtukasAndFunctionFieldLanglands, but that does not supply DM.0, DM.2, DM.4, DM.6 or DM.8. Therefore those section citations cannot be certified. The README retains the accepted Part II boundary and makes the missing contracts explicit; it does not invent a parent file or claim an implemented library. A clarification was requested while independent work continued. These supplier gaps need resolution before the package can be treated as dependency-complete. The finite-place contract has no verified roadmap owner in the supplied atlas: its parent entry requests an interface extension, and does not claim that DM.6 states it. That ownership must be settled with the missing parent, without treating the finite-place theorem as a supplied fact.

The parent contracts and the original three background gaps are recorded in `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/design/plan.json`; the additional integral stuffle gap belongs to PeriodsAndSpecialValues §PS.9.2. No notion was moved from a later roadmap and no later roadmap is used as a supplier.

## Duplication and ownership sweep

The sweep used `roadmaps grep` over main and open heads, object-oriented searches of the pinned Mathlib and Tau Ceti, and all board packages. Relevant open-head comparisons were read using `roadmaps show`, rather than treating the board copy as the head: PeriodsAndSpecialValues §PS.9.1–§PS.9.4, FunctionFieldArithmetic Layers 0–5 and GlobalShtukasAndFunctionFieldLanglands Layers 0–3. The first two board copies differ from their open heads, so the cited sections were re-resolved at the heads. Generic analytic ownership was checked against RestrictedPowerSeries §§0.1–0.3, 1.1–1.2, 2.1 and 7.2, and the native Tau Ceti restricted-series module.

Every removal or deferral is listed below; no target survives as a removed stub.

| Object | Disposition and exact owner |
|---|---|
| A second positive fixed-weight index carrier, weight and depth fields | Deleted as new constructions; use `Composition`, `Composition.blocks`, `Composition.length`, `Composition.blocksFun` in `Mathlib/Combinatorics/Enumerative/Composition.lean:101`. The new prefix order, admissibility, arrays and special families remain here. |
| A second generic composition count/finiteness proof | Deleted; use `composition_card` in `Mathlib/Combinatorics/Enumerative/Composition.lean:1033`. Weighted and restricted family counts are new here. |
| A second polynomial, monic-polynomial or rational-function carrier | Deleted; `Polynomial` in `Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Basic.lean:69`, `Polynomial.Monic` in `Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Degree/Defs.lean:59`, `RatFunc` in `Mathlib/FieldTheory/RatFunc/Defs.lean:67`. |
| A second infinity valuation and completion | Deleted; `RatFunc.inftyValuation` and `RatFunc.CompletionAtInfty` in `Mathlib/FieldTheory/RatFunc/Valuation.lean:81` and line 138. The norm normalization and the multiple series use these carriers. |
| A second free index-module or span construction | Deleted; `Finsupp` in `Mathlib/Data/Finsupp/Defs.lean:88`, `Submodule.span` in `Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Span/Defs.lean:46`. Only the q-shuffle operation and its function-field realisations remain new. |
| Generic binomial coefficients and finite-field sums of unit powers | Deleted as new APIs; `Nat.choose` in `Mathlib/Data/Nat/Choose/Basic.lean:50`, `FiniteField.sum_pow_units` in `Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean:290`. Chen's filtered coefficient is new. |
| Generic positive-index harmonic product, concatenation and their coefficient-independent unit/commutativity/weight/depth laws | Imported from PeriodsAndSpecialValues §PS.9.2, with the integral coefficient extension recorded as gap:integral-positive-index-stuffle. Deleted as new construction targets in §1.3. The characteristic-p q-shuffle correction differs and remains here. |
| A generic normed Tate algebra and a generic entire-series carrier | Imported from Tau Ceti `TauCeti/RingTheory/PowerSeries/TateAlgebra.lean` and RestrictedPowerSeries §7.2; no fresh general analytic carrier is planned. The arithmetic finite-extension condition and ABP interface are parent contracts. |
| A separate forward finite-field coefficient Frobenius for the two-row signature | Use `FiniteField.frobeniusAlgHom` in `Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean:333` and `Polynomial.mapRingHom` in `Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Eval/Defs.lean:533`. No wrapper declaration added. |

The retained multiple series have polynomial tuples, degree characters and a nonarchimedean completion, whereas the classical MZVs in PeriodsAndSpecialValues use integer tuples and a real/complex analytic carrier. The divisor zeta function of FunctionFieldArithmetic and shtuka cohomology of GlobalShtukasAndFunctionFieldLanglands are different mathematical objects. No competing owner was found for Carlitz multiple series, the q-correction, binary-relation operators, Anderson–Thakur deformation systems, X_w or the characteristic-p basis and relation theorems. No fully qualified declaration from another readable roadmap is reused.

The pin identities were verified directly: content main `7bd35ba3a6197cb1fadd5dac74e67499aa22b3b4`, Mathlib `6b7abb3c7686292736be2955bd3eb9ebf63b456a`, Tau Ceti `a91d3aafa8cd3e6bc33dfde0d7677ed0f1625039`. The design's older baseline hashes were not used to infer present APIs.

Native-definition audit: `Composition` has the fields `blocks_pos : ∀ {i}, i ∈ blocks → 0 < i` and `blocks_sum : blocks.sum = n`; it imposes no zeta convergence or real admissibility condition. `Polynomial.Monic` is `leadingCoeff p = 1`; over a zero ring this would not exclude zero, which is why the series use a field. `Submodule.span` is the infimum of submodules containing the given set. `RatFunc.CompletionAtInfty` is the infinity-uniform-space completion and derives field and rational-function algebra structure; its valuation is multiplicative integer-valued, not the normalized real norm. The native restricted subring supplies a radius condition and coefficient-ring instances, not the arithmetic finiteness condition of E. These definitions were read at the file/line locators above.

## Sources

All 19 manifest works were obtained through the sources service from legal repositories or author pages. The sources service check returned `packages/DrinfeldModulesAndTModulesPartII/sources.toml: 0 problem(s)`. The 14 design works keep their original keys; AT90, AT09, SHEATS98, CM21 and CCM-ALG supply the additional background. Each entry has authors, title, year, exact locator, cited targets and the service's retrieval/hash/licence/status fields. Successful retrievals cleared the temporary missing-reference requests; there is no outstanding missing text.

All prose is newly composed mathematical specification or retained authorial design text; no source passage or extraction was copied into the package or report. Locators are to the exact retrieved preprints. The source corrections retained from the accepted design are scoped to those versions; no unseen published typesetting is claimed. AT90's equation extraction omits some displayed symbols, so the interpolation formula was also read in AT09 §2.4.1, AT09 lines 200–222, and CHANG14 Theorem 5.5.1, CHANG14 lines 911–930. The underlying power-sum generating-series supplier requirement remains explicit.

## Adversarial mathematics pass

The tables below record finite probes and negative controls, not proofs of the universal roadmap targets. All arithmetic is in the displayed characteristic; u=θ⁻¹. Composition counts, weighted array counts, Chen coefficients and the truncated Laurent coefficients were recomputed in scratch. The Laurent test uses precision 42 and its weight-three multiplication comparison is certified only below u³⁸, because multiplying by D₁² lowers precision by four. No executable computation is part of the roadmap.

Each top-level statement has its own row. The subsequent rows expand each accepted API assertion, proof-route assertion and retained Check, rather than treating a target-level verdict as a substitute for them. Several assertions about one object share its finite witness; their universal hypotheses were separately compared with the source and the explicit Lean binders. Supplier-dependent universal claims remain supplier contracts.

| Statement | Instances tried | Computed values on the two sides / negative control | Result | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MZ.0/index — Indices, arrays and their orders | q=2; (1,2),(2,1),(1,3),(2,1,1),∅ | Prefix sums (1,3) ≤ (2,3); reverse first inequality 2≤1 is false. At weight four, (1,4,4) and (2,3,4) cross. wt(∅)=dep(∅)=0. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.0/power-sum — Power sums of both realisations | q=2,3; d=−1,0,1 | S₀(1)=Si₀(1)=1; S₋₁(1)=0; S_{<−1}(∅)=1. At q=2, S₁(1)=1/(θ²+θ); at q=3, S₁(−1;1)=−S₁(1). | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.0/multiple-zeta-value — Thakur multiple zeta values | q=2; ∅,(1),(1,1) | ζ(∅)=1; ζ(1)=1+u²+u³+…; ζ(1,1)=u²+u³+…. ζ(1)² and ζ(2) both start 1+u⁴+u⁶, u=θ⁻¹. | Agrees on probes | Native carrier and completion binder explicit. |
| MZ.0/alternating-mzv — Harada’s alternating multiple zeta values | q=2,3; ε=1,−1 | At q=2, ∣Fˣ∣=1. At q=3, ζ(−1;1)−ζ(1) starts u³, whereas forgetting ε gives zero. | Agrees on probes | Native carrier and completion binder explicit. |
| MZ.0/mzv-nonvanishing — Thakur’s nonvanishing theorem | q=2; depths one and two | The minimal tuples are (0) and (1,0). Their grouped leading values are 1 and S₁(1)=1/(θ²+θ), of valuations 0 and 2; every competing tuple has larger valuation. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |

For s=4, the two leading terms are 2u⁶ and u¹²; the equality fails outside s≤q. | Agrees on probes | Added exact θ⁻⁶ negative control. |

At q=4 the j=1,2 values vanish because 3 divides neither. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |

The residue transition determinants are ±1, hence are nonzero; the family sizes are 2 and 6. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.2/strong-brown — The strong Brown theorem for alternating polylogarithms | q=3; weight 4 | The AT family has 48 rows; the contracted AS family has 46 elements. Rows may repeat their left value, but the coefficient matrix has diagonal residues ±1 and off-diagonal residues 0 modulo D₁; its determinant residue is ±1. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.2/next-weight-relation — The next-weight relation ζ_A(2q − 1) + D_1 ζ_A(1, 2q − 2) = 0 | q=2,3; d=0; q=3,weight5 | At d=0 the two terms are 1 and D₁/ℓ₁=−1, giving 0. At q=3 the indices (5) and (1,4) have residues 2 and (1,1) mod3, so both lie in IND₅ and give a genuine obstruction. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/anderson-thakur-polynomials — Anderson–Thakur polynomials and Carlitz factorials | q=3; n=1,3,4 | H₁=H₃=1; Γ₄=D₁. H₄=2t³−t−θ³ has θ-degree 3≤(4−1)·3/2=9/2. Using H₀ for exponent 0 instead of 1 would shift the interpolation by one. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/anderson-thakur-interpolation — Anderson–Thakur interpolation of power sums | q=3; s=1,2; d=0,1 | At d=0, H_s=Γ_s=1 gives π̃⁻ˢ on both sides. At d=1,s=2, both sides are π̃⁻²ℓ₁⁻². The character γ²=−1 adds γ(−1)¹ on both sides. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/deformation-series — Chang’s deformation series | q=3; (s,Q)=((2,2),(1,θ⁴)); empty tuple | The two normalized Gauss exponents are −3 and +1; along degrees d₁=d₂+1 their exponent sum is −9·3^{d₂}+3^{d₂}=−8·3^{d₂}, while the tail alone has +3^{d₂}. The empty value is 1. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/deformation-entire — Entireness of Chang’s series | q=2; s=(1),Q=1; weak bound counterexample above | At θ the value is −1/D₁, and at θ² it is 1/D₁². Prefix shifting of a length-k tuple by r−k changes its normalized norm to its 2^{r−k}-th power times a fixed nonzero constant; a divergent tail has no such implication. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/deformation-specialisation — Specialisations of Chang’s series | q=2; s=(1),Q=1; j=0,1 | 𝓛(1)(θ)=−1/D₁ and 𝓛(1)(θ²)=1/D₁²=(−1/D₁)². Forgetting the twist of coefficients would instead evaluate the untransformed denominator at θ². | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/index-dual-motive — The dual t-motives attached to an index | depth 0,1,2; s=(1),(2,1) | At depth zero Φ=(1), determinant 1. At depth one the lower-left entry is Q^{(−1)}(t−θ), not Q^{(−1)}. At (2,1), determinant exponent is 3+1=4. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/mz-property — Chang’s MZ property | q=2; Z=Li(1),0; square of ζ(q−1) | For Li(1), ψ(θ)=(π̃⁻¹,−1/D₁); at θ², the first entry is 0 and the last is 1/D₁². Z=0 is excluded. Squaring a weight-(q−1) witness has weight 2(q−1), not q−1. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/mz-property-values — Zeta and polylogarithm values have the MZ property | q=3; s=(2),Q=1; characters 1 and −1 | The witness has determinant (t−θ)² and ψ(θ)=(π̃⁻²,−1/D₁). With total character −1, γ²=−1 and γ∉F₃, so the normalizing scalar is outside K; retaining γLi fixes c=1. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/chang-descent — Chang’s descent theorem and the graded algebra of values | q=2; weights 0 and 1; q=3 small basis | ζ(1)−1 has leading term u², not 0, so the constant and weight-one term cannot be conflated. At q=3, the two weight-two indices are (2),(1,1); the extension-of-scalars target keeps both, giving rank 2. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/prefix-system — The merged prefix system of a putative relation | weight3 pairs (1,2),(3) and (1,1,1),(1,2) | The first prefix set is {∅,(1)} of size 2, giving a 3×3 Φ and 4-entry augmented vector. The second is {∅,(1),(1,1)} of size 3, with shared (1) counted once. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/independence-criterion — The ABP independence criterion for zeta-type periods | q=2; ζ(2)+D₁ζ(1,1)=0 | The normalized full sum is f_∅(θ)=0; at prefix (1), f_{(1)}(θ)=D₁Li(1)/π̃=1. A nonzero b in F₂[t] gives b(θ)≠0 by transcendence; b=0 would give an invalid 0/0. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.3/eulerian-tails — The Eulerian suffix constraint | q=2,3; nonzero and cancelled suffix sums | At q=2 every integer is divisible by 1. At q=3 a tail of weight 1 is forbidden when its sum is nonzero; duplicate coefficients 1 and −1 on the same tail give 0, so no divisibility follows until support is collected. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/frobenius-system — The Frobenius difference systems (E_M) and their solution spaces | w=0; q=3,w=2,3,4 | X₀ consists of θ-constant polynomials over F(t). At q=3,w=2, the prefix tree is {∅,(2)}; at w=3 it has 3 vertices, and at w=4 it has 4. Outside-tree coordinates equal 0 in both the native and localized presentations. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/monic-frobenius-descent — Monic Frobenius descent | F₂⊂F₄; positive versus zero degree | For degree zero F=G=1, α²=α+1 gives a solution outside F₂[t]. For positive degree F=t and constant δ=α, the coefficient of t on the right is α but on the left is 0, excluding that wrong model. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/theta-degree-bound — The θ-degree bound for solutions | q=3,w=2; leaf and root | The generator root t³−θ has θ-degree 1=2/(3−1); its leaf t−t³ has degree 0. A proposed root θ² gives left degree 6 and right maximal degree 4, so it cannot solve the system. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/key-lemma — The key lemma: relations give solutions of (E_M) | q=2; weight1 period relation | Li(1)=−π̃/D₁. The solution with root t²−θ and leaf t−t² satisfies root twist t²−θ²=(t−θ)²=(root+leaf)(t−θ). Its leaf is the coefficient after a common nonzero denominator is cleared. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/solution-space-dimension — The dimension of the solution space X_w | q=3; w=1,2; ℓ=7,m=2,n=1 | At w=1, a=(a+b)(t−θ) forces a=b=0. At w=2, root t³−θ and leaf t−t³ give t³−θ³ on both sides. The matrix count at ℓ=7 is 7+5=12, whereas 2(ℓ−m)=10. | Agrees on probes | Defined m and corrected block counts; full descended carrier. |
| MZ.4/twisted-system-solutions — Solutions of the character-twisted system | q=3; ε=−1 and ε=1; q=2 reduced support | At a divisible root the leading-coefficient comparison is εf=f. With ε=−1 it reads 2f=f, forcing f=0. With ε=1 it reads f=f, leaving at most one parameter. At q=2 there is just one character. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.4/two-row-system — Ngo Dac’s two-row system in weights q < w ≤ 2q − 2 | q=3,k=2,w=4; a=1 | Set a₁=t³−t, a₂=δ₂=0, δ₁=(t−θ)⁶. Put Y=θ³,A=t³−t. The first forward-twisted row reduces on both sides to t²+ tY+Y²; (t−Y)²−(t³−Y)²=A(−t³−t−Y). The second row is 0=0. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/small-entry-independence — Independence of small-entry multiple zeta values | q=2; w=1,2,3 | I⁰_w consists of the all-one tuple. The leading valuations of its values are 0,2,8, respectively; hence each singleton family is nonzero. A zero coefficient is not a supported singleton relation. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/thakur-basis-small-weight — Thakur’s basis through weight q | q=2,w=2; q=3,w=1,2,3 | The basis cardinalities are 1 and 1,2,3. At w=q the singleton (q) is excluded, so 2^{q−1}−1 gives 1 for q=2 and 3 for q=3. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/q-indivisible-independence — Independence of q-indivisible MZVs through weight 2q − 2 | q=3; w=4 versus w=5 | IND₄ has 6 compositions. At w=5 the nonzero relation has indices (5),(1,4), both q-indivisible, with coefficients 1,D₁; it prevents extension past 2q−2=4. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/theorem-d — Thakur’s basis through weight 2q − 2 | q=2,w=2; q=3,w=4 | At q=2 the range ends at q=2 with one basis value. At q=3,w=4 the explicit Thakur family has 6 indices and the independent IND family also has 6, so the dimension squeeze is 6≤dim≤6. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/cmpl-ind-basis — The CMPL basis indexed by IND_w | q=2,w=0,3; q=3,w=4 | At w=0 the value is 1 and the rank is 1. At q=2,w=3 the indices are (3),(1,1,1), count 2; at q=3,w=4 the count is 6. The ordinary MZV IND family instead fails at q=3,w=5. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/thakur-basis-theorem — Thakur’s basis theorem and Todd’s dimension formula | q=2,w=0..5; q=3,w=4,5 | The predicted dimensions are 1,1,1,2,3,5 and 6,11. At q=3,w=4 the spanning and independent counts are both 6. The empty family consists of one value 1, not an empty vector family. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/all-relations — All linear relations among multiple zeta values | q=2,w=2 and w=3 | At w=2, 2 compositions minus 1 basis index gives kernel dimension 1, generated by [2]−D₁[1,1]. At w=3, 4 compositions minus 2 basis indices gives dimension 2; the rewrite residuals have realization 0. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/character-splitting — Character splitting of relations among ACMPL periods | q=3; roots 1 and γ with γ²=−1 | The polynomial X²+1 has values 1,2,2 at 0,1,2, so γ∉F₃. The two constant roots give independent constant directions; repeating the character 1 instead gives the dependent pair (1,1). | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/acmpl-period-relations — Relations among normalised ACMPL periods | q=2,w=1; q=3,w=1,2 | At q=2, 1+D₁𝓛(1)(θ)=1−1=0. At q=3,w=1, 2 does not divide 1, so the constant coefficient must vanish. At w=2, 1+D₁𝓛(2)(θ)=0 is the normalized constant-one relation. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/hoffman-basis — The Hoffman-like basis of alternating polylogarithm values | q=3; w=1,4 | The weight-one family has 2 arrays, Li(1;1) and Li(−1;1). At weight four the depth-weighted array count is 46; the weak generating family has 48, so it is not a basis. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/amzv-dimension — The dimension of alternating multiple zeta values | q=2,3; w=1,4 | At q=2 all characters are 1, so AZ_w=Z_w. At q=3, s(1)=2 and s(4)=46; the ordinary counts are d(1)=1 and d(4)=6, distinguishing the coefficient and character spaces. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/mzv-lower-bound-cmpl — The lower bound dim_K Z_w ≥ d(w) through CMPLs | q=3,w=4; trivial versus arbitrary characters | The 6 trivial-character IND values lie in Z₄, giving the count 6. The full alternating family has 46 values and only gives membership in AZ₄; substituting that space would not prove a lower bound for Z₄. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/amzv-small-weight-bounds — Direct bounds for alternating MZVs in small weights | q=3,w=4,5,6; q=2 boundary | The direct family weighted counts are 46,130,368. At w=5, (5) is excluded and (2,3) is admitted with character one over 3. At w=6, (2,3,1) and (1,2,3) are admitted, (3,2,1) is excluded. q=2,w=4 has dimension d(4)=3. | Agrees on probes | Retained hypotheses and exact support. |
| MZ.5/v-adic-bound — An upper bound for v-adic multiple zeta values | q=2,w=1; q=3,w=1,2,4 | Bounds are 1−1=0; 1−0=1; 2−1=1; 6−2=4. At w=q−1 the lost space has dimension 1. A nonzero K̄-span has infinite K-dimension, so the two coefficient-field conventions cannot be mixed. | Agrees on probes | Corrected algebra-map source; finite-place supplier gap explicit. |

The following conventions and native definitions were also tested independently of a target's main theorem.

| Statement | Instances tried | Computed values / negative control | Result | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q is a finite-field cardinal, not the characteristic | F₄ versus F₂; q=1 | |F₄|=4 while p=2; entry 2 has residue 2 mod4 and 0 mod2. A field has at least 0≠1, so q=1 is excluded. | Agrees | Explicit finite-field carrier. |
| Valuation/norm direction | θ, θ⁻¹, 1 | v∞ values −1,1,0 and norms q,q⁻¹,1. Reversing the valuation exchanges q and q⁻¹. | Agrees | Native valuation distinguished from normalized norm. |
| ℓ₁ versus D₁ | q=3 | ℓ₁+D₁=0, ℓ₁−D₁=2ℓ₁≠0. | Agrees | Signed denominator fixed. |
| Coefficient Frobenius fixes t | a+bt and t+θ | Twist aᑫ+bᑫt and t+θᑫ; whole q-th power aᑫ+bᑫtᑫ differs. | Agrees | Native coefficient map named. |
| Period normalization | q=3,w=1,2 | v∞(π̃)=−3/2, v∞(π̃²)=−3; ζ(2)/π̃²=−1/D₁. | Agrees | Nonzero denominators stated. |
| Matrix direction | depth one | Second row of Φψ is Q^{(−1)}(t−θ)Ω+𝔏; deleting t−θ leaves Q^{(−1)}Ω+𝔏. | Agrees | Essential subdiagonal retained. |
| Shifted H and Γ indexing | q=3 | H₁=H₃=1, Γ₄=D₁, H₄=2t³−t−θ³; H₀ belongs to the other indexing convention. | Agrees | Indices n≥1 explicit. |
| Empty index and zero module boundaries | w=0; Fin 0 | One polynomial tuple, one degree tuple, empty products 1; Z₀=K·1, dimension 1. The zero span would have dimension 0. | Agrees | No zero-depth exclusion. |
| Zero-polynomial degree and zero ring | p=0; field versus zero ring | natDegree(0)=0 but degree(0)=−∞; leadingCoeff(0)=0≠1 over a field. In a zero ring 0=1, so monicity alone is insufficient. | Agrees | Extended degree convention and Field hypotheses. |
| Finite-place zero/negative weight conventions | q=3,w=1,2 | d(−1)=0, d(0)=1; bounds 1−0=1 and 2−1=1. Natural subtraction without the separate negative convention would use the wrong d(0) in weight one. | Agrees | Integer-indexed sequence extension. |
| Native X_w prefix and residual definitions | q=3,w=0,1,2 | At w=0 only ∅ survives; f∅(θ³)=f∅(θ) forces θ-degree 0. At w=1 the equations force (a,b)=(0,0). At w=2 root t³−θ and leaf t−t³ give residual 0; any outside coordinate has residual equal to itself. | Agrees | No degree truncation, genuine kernel. |
| Native direct-family adjacency | q=3,w=6; length 0,1 | (2,3,1),(1,2,3) each have exactly one required adjacent pair; (3,2,1) has zero. Fin(length−1) is empty at lengths 0 and 1, so no out-of-range entry is used. | Agrees | Adjacent-position domain explicit. |

| Statement | Instances tried | Computed values / negative control | Result | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Empty and out-of-range collapse | ∅, (1,2), i=1,3 | T₁(∅)=∅, T₃(1,2)=(3); the raw sum-at-empty formula gives (0), of weight 0 and depth 1, which is not a positive composition. | Corrected | Empty case and min(i,depth) specified. |
| Full CMPL convergence domain | q=3,s=(2,2),z=(1,θ⁴); z₂=0 | c₁=3⁻³,c₂=3; along adjacent degrees the exponent is −8·3^{d₂}, so the tuple converges. The tail exponent is +3^{d₂}, so that tail diverges. With z₂=0 the tuple is zero regardless of z₁. | Agrees | Quantified full domain and nonzero-prefix condition explicit. |
| Empty merged pair family | n=0,w=1 | I={∅}, det Φ=t−θ, f=v=0; ΦΨ and Ψ^{(−1)} both equal diag((t−θ)Ω,1). | Agrees | Root retained in an empty union. |

| Statement | Instances tried | Computed values / negative control | Result | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Inverse Frobenius needs a perfect coefficient field | t+θ over K and K̄ | v∞(θ)=−1, while v∞(xᑫ)=qv∞(x) for x∈K; θ^{1/q} has value −1/q and is outside K. In K̄ the inverse twist gives t+θ^{1/q}, and its forward twist is t+θ. | Agrees | The inverse twist is confined to K̄. |
| Prime power versus prime in degree-one sums | q=4,p=2 | The product over F₄ is θ⁴−θ, its derivative is 1, and the log derivative is S₁(1)=1/(θ⁴−θ). Its valuation is 4, not 2. | Agrees | Added an actual q=4 power-sum witness. |
| Summed relation does not imply a binary relation | empty pair ([∅],−[∅]) | R(−1)=−1,R(0)=1, so the family is not identically zero although its summed value is 1−1=0. | Agrees | Both integer cutoff directions retained. |

| Statement | Instances tried | Computed values / negative control | Result | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Finite dimension versus total natural finrank | zero K-module and K[X], K=F₃(t) | The zero module has finrank 0 and is finite. K[X] has the independent family 1,X,X²,… of every finite length, hence infinite rank and native finrank 0 by Cardinal.toNat; it is not finite. | Corrected signature | Added Module.Finite together with the finrank equality; definition read at Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Finrank.lean:61. |

## Expanded API, route and Check probes

Every row below refers to a particular statement from the accepted catalogue and uses the corresponding computed witness above. Generic declarations deleted in favor of suppliers are marked as such; the row records their supplier comparison rather than a new target. No claim is inferred merely from a similarly named declaration.

At weight four, (1,4,4) and (2,3,4) cross. wt(∅)=dep(∅)=0. | Agrees on probes | Witness and hypotheses compared. |

At weight four, (1,4,4) and (2,3,4) cross. wt(∅)=dep(∅)=0. | Agrees on probes | Witness and hypotheses compared. |

At weight four, (1,4,4) and (2,3,4) cross. wt(∅)=dep(∅)=0. | Agrees on probes | Witness and hypotheses compared. |

At weight four, (1,4,4) and (2,3,4) cross. wt(∅)=dep(∅)=0. | Agrees on probes | Witness and hypotheses compared. |

| MZ.0/counting Route 1: Split an element of IND_w by its first entry: 1 ≤ s_1 ≤ q − 1 leaves an element of IND_{w−s_1}, and s_1 > q gives (s_1 − q, s_2, …) ∈ IND_{w−q}; so ∣IND_w∣ = Σ_{i=1}^{q} ∣IND_{w−i}∣ for w > q, with the initial values counted directly (Ngo Dac Lemma 6.1(1)). | q=3,w=4,5,6 | (∣Iᵀ∣,∣IND∣,∣AS∣,∣AT∣)=(6,6,46,48),(11,11,130,140),(20,20,368,408). The weighted depth sum in weight four is 16+24+4+2=46. | Witness agrees; parent input unresolved | Witness and hypotheses compared. |

| MZ.3/deformation-entire Route 4: For the weaker whole-tuple condition put c_j = ‖Q_j‖/∣θ∣^{q s_j/(q−1)} > 0. To test a prefix of length k, shift all its summation indices by r−k and append the fixed indices r−k−1, …, 0. The whole-tuple norm is a fixed positive constant times the q^{r−k}-th power of the prefix norm. Thus decay for the whole tuple implies decay for every prefix. This puts the displayed first-column vector in T, and the same equation and DM.8/entire-from-equation put it in E; convergence of the other columns is unnecessary (Chang Lemma 5.3.1, proof, p. 15). | q=2; s=(1),Q=1; weak bound counterexample above | At θ the value is −1/D₁, and at θ² it is 1/D₁². | MZ.5/acmpl-period-relations Route 2: Work by strong induction on this relations theorem for the normalised periods, with distinct arrays and nonzero collected coefficients. Character splitting leaves one total character. The absence of homogeneous relations in smaller weight gives (LW) for the prefix periods. For each nonempty prefix, the independence criterion makes its suffix combination a scalar in K; the induction hypothesis applied to that lower-weight relation, including its constant term, forces every supported suffix character to be trivial and its weight divisible by q − 1. Varying the prefix therefore gives later characters 1 and later entries divisible by q − 1. This uses the full induction hypothesis, beyond the valuation conclusion of MZ.3/eulerian-tails; that lemma also gives a = 0 when (q − 1) ∤ w. | q=2,w=1; q=3,w=1,2 | At q=2, 1+D₁𝓛(1)(θ)=1−1=0. The main negative controls are nontrivial q=4 coefficients, empty and negative cutoffs, q=2 and q=3 signs, omitted character factors, an entry q+1, repeated support with cancelling coefficients, an invalid full tail matrix, a zero determinant, a zero denominator, degree-zero monic descent, a false zero-solution assertion at the two-row endpoint, and confusing K- with K̄-dimension. Nonabelian-group tests, arbitrary fans, nonreduced schemes, mixed-characteristic DVR formulas and compact-group conditions do not occur in any statement of this package; there is no untested generic group or geometric-carrier claim concealed in those directions.
